Stefan Tisel, Milenko Ziherl from 2 p.m. on, Janko Weber, Rudolf Petan from 17.30 p.m., Sam Bevk from 13 to 18 p.m., Franc Jurša and Roberto Battelli. I say hello to everyone! We're moving to 2. Agenda item – Defence Proposal for a Law on Supplements to the Law on Digital Radiodifusion. The draft law was tabled by the Government for consideration by the National Assembly. I give the floor to the Minister of Higher Education, Science and Technology for the introduction of the bill. Here you go, Mr. Golobich.
ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u2	Mr President, Members and Members of Parliament! Allow me to briefly present the content and reasons for this amendment to the law, which has been tabled under urgent procedure. In October 2007, the Digital Broadcasting Act, which regulates a very important area, was adopted in this High Council, namely the transition from analogue to digital broadcasting. This transition is crucial in order to release the spectrum on which we will be able to develop broadband services in the future and to ensure that the largest area of the Republic of Slovenia is covered by this service. Our ambition is that it becomes a universal service. The law also set the final date after which radio broadcasting in analogue technology will no longer be allowed in Slovenia, i.e. the date after which it should be available only in digital technology. This date was set on 1 December 2010, the date approaching. The law laid down the procedure for the State to establish the construction of so-called multiplexes, so-called networks allowing individual operators to transmit a digital signal. One of these networks was allocated to national television and it was stipulated for this network that, at least 6 months before the start of exclusively digital broadcasting, it must provide coverage to residents with signals, i.e. public broadcasting programmes, to a extent not less than 95 %. In implementing this law, we have in the meantime received an APEK warning that in some narrowly defined geographically rounded areas with December 1st, this cut-off date, problems could arise that, for certain technical reasons, RTV Slovenia would not be able to ensure coverage of these small areas with a digital signal. The coverage to be provided with a high degree of certainty is expected to exceed 90% but could be questioned by these 95%. In short, to avoid a situation in which, on the one hand, the APEK would be forced to act against the operator of this first multiplex, and to avoid any illegal situation and, on the other hand, to avoid a situation where a certain part of the population of the Republic of Slovenia would not be covered by RTV Slovenia, i.e. by this public service, we are proposing this novelty, which, in the event that RTV Slovenia itself, together with the APEK, will find that, within a period available at the time when only the digital signal will be available, in such areas, will not be able to provide a digital signal that, in such cases, the special role of national APEK will allow, exceptionally, to extend the transmission of the signal only in those areas in analogic techniques for the period until the end of June next year, i.e. for six months. This law, therefore, allows action in a situation that will not necessarily occur, but there is a possibility that it will happen and we want to avoid the unloving consequences in terms of respect for the law and in terms of the provision of this service to citizens and citizens. That's why it's an emergency procedure, so right now. We have participated in a meeting of the committee that has supported this law, including those that take account of the amendment of the coalition parliamentary groups, the Government supports and proposes that this amendment should solve the problem that has been pointed out in the most appropriate way. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u12	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Dear Minister, colleagues and colleagues! The proposed Law on Complementation of the Digital Broadcasting Act seeks to prevent the possibility and risk that some geographically rounded areas will not be covered by RTV Slovenia programmes in digital technology due to a certain date of transition from analogue broadcasting to digital, i.e. from 1 December 2010. It is a danger that some residents will lose their television signal and consequently lose their right to be informed by the media. Therefore, timely adoption of an appropriate legal solution is necessary to maintain the analogue signal or television in force until the relevant date, as proposed, by 30 June 2011, which is why the proposal for the adoption of the law is based on an urgent procedure. We are all aware of the benefits of digital broadcasting and of the greater use of radio spectrum in digital technology, greater opportunities for offering and using new services and many other advantages. The proposed law thus complements the radio spectrum regulation in accordance with the Law on Electronic Communications and regulates individual digital broadcasting related technical issues, i.e. the gradual transition from analogue to digital broadcasting. The law will allow APEC to decide which analogue converters will be able to operate until this extended deadline, that is until 30 June 2011. Unfortunately, such a proposal would also cause a lot of discomfort to users, including in areas not defined as areas of white fox, as there are many examples of simultaneous use of TV apparatus on cable and also on the antenna, not to mention cases where there is no cable connection. Dissatisfaction will also be with traders and so on, and it would therefore make sense to extend such a period of time by perhaps a maximum of 24 months. It would therefore make sense to extend this period of extinguishing of the analogue signal to other geographically rounded areas, i.e. also those areas which are not designated as white foxes, so that broadcasters can extend the programme in a particular geographical area simultaneously in digital and analogue techniques, at most, 24 months from the beginning of the simultaneous analogue and digital broadcasting period. However, the phase-out should start at least 6 months before the end of the simultaneous broadcasting period. This would make sense, because otherwise there will be problems, as I mentioned earlier, and in the further debate on any amendments tabled, the matter could be debated in substance. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u26	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, Members and Members of Parliament! With the last amendment to the Excise Act of July 2010, the Government proposed new excise amounts, including electricity and natural gas, in order to ensure the stability of public finances when preparing the budget balance. Despite the fact that raising excise duty amounts means a certain impact on the individual consumer and those economic operators who do not meet the legal conditions for the exemption of excise duty because the share of energy costs in the final value of their product is less than 50%, the Government considered that the proposed amendments at that time constitute an appropriate compromise between the various objectives. Following the entry into force of the law, the Ministry of Finance and the Ministry of Economy continued discussions with representatives of representative associations of the economy and weighed possible different solutions. These discussions highlighted some of the broader problems faced by the electricity or natural gas industry in manufacturing processes. The amount of excise duty is one of these segments. That is why we have agreed to continue discussions in various areas and to look for the best possible solutions. The first result of these talks is today in the form of a new compromise solution on the amount of electricity excise duty or natural gas. In fact, the proposed law retains the current excise duty for both of the mentioned energy at an unaltered level. However, the electricity excise duty provided for for on 1 November 2010 will not take place as a result of the adoption of this Act. At the time of the preparation of the previous amendment of the Law, the Government stressed that it was an intervention measure and would closely monitor the situation with regard to electricity and natural gas prices and propose amendments to the measures adopted, as appropriate. Following a re-examination of the situation, both in terms of the requirements of economic operators and also in terms of the impact that the anticipated increase in excise duties would have on inflation, the Government has decided to propose to the National Assembly to adopt a change to the Excise Law that is before you. We estimate that, due to the proposed law, the revenue of the state budget on excise duties will be lower than originally planned, and in 2010 about €8 million, and in 2011 about € 103 million, and this new estimate of the revenue of the State budget for 2011 is already taken into account in the proposal for a revised budget to be considered in the short term. Your Honor! It takes into account the reasons and effects that we want to achieve with the proposed bill. We propose to the National Assembly that the proposal for a new Law on excise duties be confirmed. Thank you very much.

In the debate, the two amendments, as part of their additional explanations, pointed out, inter alia, that the Government's behaviour was unacceptable, which, in the context of the previous amendment of the excise law, ensured that the economy would have no negative consequences, and now the law is being amended for this very reason. Following its provision, discussions will also continue on the possibility of selective action in the field of excise duty in certain segments of the economy, as provided for by the European Directive. The amendment would result in a further reduction in the budget revenue which is not planned in the proposed budgets for the coming years and would result in a discrepancy between the excise law and the budget adopted. Radovan Žerjava and the amendment of Article 6 of the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group. The Committee voted on all articles of the bill and adopted them. ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u29	Thank you very much. The following is a presentation of the views of the groups of Members. There was a mag in the word. Borut Saiovic, who will not present his position. Mr Bogdan Čepič has the floor, on behalf of the Group of Social Democrats.
ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u30	Thank you, again, for your word. The Parliamentary Group of Social Democrats will support the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Excise. Notwithstanding the fact that excise duties paid by energy, alcohol and tobacco are an extremely important revenue and reliable source of the budget, the Government has decided, on the basis of comments and has warned, in particular from the economy, that no other harmonisation of excise duties, as planned, has been carried out. The proposed measure aims, irrespective of the additional need for budgetary revenues, to reduce the negative consequences of the financial and economic crisis, to reduce the amounts of electricity consumption adopted. In order to achieve the second step of the increase in excise duty to be carried out from 1 November 2010, an excessive burden on the Slovenian economy and a significant deterioration in the competitiveness of Slovenian companies could be achieved, and it is therefore essential that the proposed law be enforced before 1 November 2010. The question may be asked whether you did not know this before, and we have all warned you about this, including with the amendments, it has been known and the budget revenues were planned. However, I trust the Government's representatives who explained to the Committee on Finance that this reduction was decided primarily on the basis of the strong arguments put forward by the economy. The government decided not to carry out any other harmonisation and to remain excise duties as set out on 1 August 2010. This decision will also result in an increase in the budget deficit, estimated at 0.3% of GDP, as it is not possible to replace this revenue deficit with other revenues. The proposed solutions determine a excise duty on electricity of 3,05 euros per megawatt hour [[MWh]] , that is for business and non-business use. In addition, the amount of excise duty on natural gas used for heating of 0,018 euros per cubic metre and a transitional period of up to 1 May 2014 shall be determined, including the amount of excise duty on natural gas for the purpose of propulsion of 0,018 euros per cubic metre. After the previous period, the excise duty on natural gas for propulsion purposes will still be 0.0981 euros per cubic metre. Thus, under the assumption that the law will be enforced in October 2010, even after 1 November 2010, when the second harmonisation of excise duty from electricity under the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on excise duty is to be carried out, the amount of excise duty should be the same as that applicable from 1 August 2010. In all other articles, the draft law contains editorial corrections aimed at bringing the applicant into line with the individual provisions of the law. As a result of the proposed lower excise duty on natural gas electricity, based on the latest change in the amount of electricity and natural gas excise duty applicable from 1 August 2010, the revenues will be lower than planned and cannot be replaced by other public financial revenues. As it is said, in the Group of Social Democrats we support the proposed changes in excise duties because we believe that fiscal consolidation must be based on a reduction in public financial expenditure, which will have little impact on economic growth than can be the case with an increase in excise duties. Of course, the proposed changes will also result in lower planned inflation in 2010 and 2011, as foreseen by the increase in excise duties, which is also, of course, positive.

ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u32	Thank you for the floor, Mr President. Nice greeting, Secretary of State! Dear colleagues and colleagues! The draft law on amendments and additions to the Law on excise duties was tabled by the Government of the Republic of Slovenia. The aim of the law is, according to the proposaler, to set excise duties at such a level that business operations are not compromised and their international competitiveness is weakened and that changes in retail electricity and natural gas prices would not affect excessive inflation growth. In doing so, we cannot go beyond the fact that the last amendment to the Law on excise duty was adopted only three months ago. At that time, we drew attention to the damaging effects of the enormous increase in excise duties on electricity and natural gas, which the economy would feel, but the government refused to listen to us. The Government is now partially correcting this error, but excise duties will still be three times higher than before the adoption of the previous amendment of the law. This, of course, means a disagreement between the government’s promises that it will not increase taxes and what it really does. This is also contrary to the words of the President of the Government, who made it clear that the increase in taxes does not lead to the consolidation of public finances. This change is intended to raise electricity excise duties from one euro to 3.5 euros per MWh in August 2010 and from 1 November to 12.1 euros per MWh. It is now a sudden change so that the excise duty on electricity is set at 3,05 euros for MWh, both for business and non-business consumption. This is still three times higher than it was before the adoption of the previous novel of the Law on Excise. It is similar to natural gas, which determines the amount of excise duty on natural gas used for heating at 0,0180 euros per cubic metre. The previous amendment adopted 0.0363 euros per cubic metre and a transitional period until 1 May 2014, in which the amount of excise duty on natural gas for propulsion purposes was also EUR 0.0180 per cubic metre. Prior to the adoption of the previous novelty, it amounted to excise duty for heating and for propulsion purposes of 0,0063 euros per cubic metre. After the transition period, the natural gas excise duty for propulsion purposes will be 0.0981 per cubic metre. In our parliamentary group, we are opposed to the policy of raising excise duty during the financial and economic crisis, and we have also proposed an amendment on the home labour body to return the amount of electricity and natural gas excise duty to the state before the last novelty of excise law was enacted, that is to say, by 1 August 2010. However, as usual, this was not accepted. However, since the increase in excise duties is still smaller than envisaged by the law currently in force, we will not oppose the amendment of the excise law in the Slovenian Democratic Party. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u58	Thank you so much for saying that. With regard to the proposed amendment, allow me some clarifications or warnings. The proposed amendment suspends the application of Article 6, paragraph 2, of the law. However, the applicant did not take into account that the second paragraph of Article 6 of the Act relating to the amendment to Article 54, paragraph 3, is substantively linked to the fourth paragraph of that Article, which deletes the fourteenth paragraph. In the basic proposal for a law, the substance of the 14th paragraph was transferred to Article 54, paragraph 3, which is why we estimate that such an amendment would lead to a substantive non-compliance in the law and therefore we cannot support it. The additional reason that leads us to the conclusion that we should suggest to the National Assembly that this amendment is not supported is of a financial nature. If this amendment were to be supported, the revenues of the State budget in 2011 would be even lower by around EUR 30 to 35 million. With regard to the question raised by Mr President. The law, which was discussed in the National Assembly in July and also in this bill, distinguishes between the consequences of the proposal for a law in 2010 and the consequences in 2011. The bill, which was adopted in July this year, took into account the financial implications of the legal amounts set out in the bill, i.e. that electricity spending would increase to €6 per MWh from 1 August 2010 and €12 per MWh from 1 November 2010 to €12 per MWh. The financial implications, as assessed in this bill, answer the question of how much less the revenue of the state budget would be due to the fact that we are not raising excise duties to EUR 12 but are leaving them at the level of EUR 3 and 5 cents per MWh. As a result, it is estimated that revenue in the state budget will be lower by good €6.6 million this year. It is taken into account that only one excise duty bill would be submitted in 2010 for the month of November and therefore the effects in 2010 due to the proposed amendment of the law are relatively low, that is, the December excise duty calculation is being invested at the end of January and will have implications for the 2011 budget. As it is said, the assessment of the financial implications of this bill is focused on clarifications as to how much revenue would be reduced in relation to the statutory amounts if the new amounts of excise duty were to be imposed. And, as it is said, this year, together for natural gas and electricity, we estimate around 8 million lower revenues, while in 2011 these revenues will be reduced by about 103 million euros. Allow me a brief explanation in relation to the applicable excise duty on 1 August 2010. It is true that the law provided for a excise duty on electricity as from 1 August 2010 to amount to EUR 6 and 5 cents per MWh. However, the Government took advantage of the mandate provided for in Article 66 of the Law on excise duty and adopted a regulation before 1 August 2010 which reduced excise duty by 50 % or 3 euros and 5 cents for MWh. In such a way that the claim in the material to maintain the excise duty level at the level of the excise duty in force on 1 August 2010 is correct by this bill. I also cannot agree with the assessment that we are raising excise duties by means of the proposed law, since we are maintaining the level of excise duty at the level already in force today, as a result of which we are assessing that the proposed measure or proposed law is not contrary to the Government's assessment of non-tax evasion in 2011. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u66	Mr President! Dear Members and Members! In short, on the main solutions of the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Value Added Tax Act. The main objective of the proposal is to transpose into the Slovenian legal order a number of recent VAT directives. First of all, it is a directive amending the value added tax directive with regard to the provision of services. These are changes relating to the location of the provision of services for cultural, artistic, sports, scientific, entertainment or similar events. In the case of admissions to these events, the taxable persons will be counted as the place where these services will actually take place, following the principle of taxation at the point of consumption. In addition, the draft law transposes into the law amendments to the VAT Directive on the prevention of tax evasion related to imports, which sets out a package of minimum conditions under which the exemption applies to imports of goods followed by delivery to another Member State. Amendments to the Directive relating to equal tax treatment of natural gas, electricity, heating and cooling are also included. The individual errors included in the original Directive are addressed. In addition, the immediate application of the right to deduct VAT in the purchase of real estate or other real estate expenditure is envisaged for the part used for business purposes where the property is intended for both business and non-business use. We are further introducing an additional measure to prevent fraud in greenhouse gas emissions trading. A temporary introduction of a reverse tax mechanism is envisaged, allowing the VAT obligation to be transferred to the person to whom greenhouse gas emission rights are transferred. In view of the end of the transitional period during which 8,5 % of the VAT rate was allowed in Slovenia from residential buildings for the market, the proposal abolishes the lower rate of taxation from those new buildings with effect from 1 January. The lower rate of taxation will continue to be applied to dwellings or dwellings which meet the criteria for treatment as housing facilities which are deemed to be part of social policy for VAT purposes. According to current data, this means that a lower VAT rate is maintained by around 95% of residential properties. The bill also includes exemption from the payment of VAT from universal postal services and from the delivery of stamps at nominal value used in Slovenia as payment for those postal services. Finally, the proposal for a law also introduces an amendment to the VAT return directive to taxable persons established in another Member State, extending the VAT refund deadline for 2009 only. This period shall be extended until 31 March 2011, due to the difficulties of some other Member States by establishing the necessary electronic data exchange system. The proposal for a law provides that the proposed amendments will be implemented as from 1 January 2011. Your Honor! Taking into account these essential solutions to the law, I suggest that you support the proposal for a law both in the second and third readings. Thank you very much.

According to the representative, the VAT regime is always up to date and is subject to many amendments. For example, more detailed arrangements for the supply of gas through distribution systems have been put in place, greater transparency has been introduced by establishing the place of provision of services related to admissions to events, the exemption of VAT on joint ventures, the exemption of VAT on the provision of universal postal services and, in particular, the abolition of the lower VAT rate on residential facilities subject to social policy. Finally, support was expressed for the amendment of coalition groups, which helps to clarify the text itself. In the context of the hearing, the Committee adopted amendments to SD, Res, LDS and DeSUS’s parliamentary groups on 1, 2, 6, 18, 19, 27, 29, 37, and Article 38 of the legislative proposal. In accordance with Article 126 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee further voted on the individual articles of the draft law and adopted them in full. In view of the amendments adopted and pursuant to Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the text of the amended law, which includes all amendments, is also prepared. The amended bill is an integral part of the report you have received.

ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u76	Thank you, Chairman. Dear Madam Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues! Allow me to present the DeSUS position on the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Value Added Tax Act. The main reason for preparing the text of this proposed amendment to the basic value added tax law is the transposition of several directives of the European Union. The tax system of the Member States of the European Union, however, falls within the national regime of each country, which also applies to the system of value added tax. In view of the diversity of the VAT system, the European Community is drawing up the common objectives of this policy, which are reflected in the Directives adopted. It is therefore only a matter of drawing up common objectives that individual countries also pursue by harmonising national rules. The path and procedure to achieve these objectives is left to the discretion of each Member State individually. In line with current practice, the Government has drawn up a new Law on VAT which aims to transpose or harmonise as many as five Directives of the European Union which relate to different areas. The first area is the exemption for the supply of natural gas and electricity. Secondly, it is more transparent to regulate tax exemptions for institutions established within the European Union. The third is for a shorter extension of the deadlines for submitting applications for VAT refund to taxable persons not established in the Member States. And the fourth is the rate of VAT on residential buildings for the market. This latter definition and the latter content in the DeSUS Group caused the most controversy, dilemma and debate. Although the last substantive amendment does not relate to the abolition of a lower VAT rate for residential buildings belonging outside residential buildings which are subject to the social policy of the State. First of all, we asked Members of the DeSUS what will be the impact of the introduction of a general rate on the real estate market itself. The answer is that such a decision will not affect the direction of further deterioration of the social situation of those who solve the housing problem. Therefore, the DeSUS Group is acceptable because those housing facilities that are part of social policy, such as dwellings for young families, dwellings that represent a more lasting solution to the housing problem, will continue to be taxed at a lower rate if they do not exceed the square space limit of 120 square metres in a multi-housing house or in an individual house of 250 square metres of usable area. In short, the solutions proposed go in the direction of appropriateness and acceptability. Nor do they deviate from the basic principles of VAT accounting, nor do they represent higher burdens for citizens and citizens. And that is the reason why we will support the novel in the DeSUS Group. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u94	Mr Vice-President, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! The proposal for a new law on pension and disability insurance responds to one of the biggest challenges facing modern societies, that is to the ageing population, where the relationship between the active and the beneficiaries of pension payments is irrevocably turned towards the latter, i.e. pensioners. This raises a question before us: Is the pensioner still a cash register and until when is it sustainable? In fact, more than a quarter of the resources needed are already contributing to the pension fund and we cannot afford all statutory payments, such as pension coordination, from it. If we do nothing, there will be a need for between 200 million and 300 million additional resources each year, and public debt would already exceed 60% of gross domestic product in 2021 on this account alone. The second issue is the issue of falling initial pensions, which is embedded in the existing system. If the average pension is now at the level of a good 60% of the average wage, this percentage would be reduced to 50% and could be below 50, which would seriously jeopardise the social situation of an increasing number of pensioners. Therefore, we have agreed with the social partners that a modernised pension and disability insurance system must ensure the achievement of two fundamental objectives: the sustainability of the pension allowance and the cessation of the fall in pensions against salaries. The set of measures that we are proposing has been prepared in cooperation with the best experts in our country. We used the knowledge that exists in this area in the wider space. On this basis, I can ensure that the proposed law ensures the sustainability of the pension system for the next 15 to 20 years and that pensions will cease to fall or will be further increased by 2 to 18%, with lower income groups higher. Among the main measures are the following: raising the age for retirement to 65 years, uniform for both sexes, and raising the age for early retirement to 60 years. we propose a new way of coordinating pensions, 60% in terms of wage growth and 40% in terms of cost of living. A new method of determining the pension base is envisaged, namely the number of years taken into account to be extended from 18 to 34, the dose rate no longer changes and remains at 80 % and we also fix the balancing factor. This will ensure that wages do not fall or increase slightly. We introduce collection of contributions from all forms of work without delay and write-off options. We exclude social transfers from the pension register, namely those transfers for which contributions to the social security system have not been paid. The new pension system provides for a whole range of benefits or special solutions for specific groups. For workers who have started working early, we assume that women can retire at 41 years of age and men at 43 years of age, they do not have to wait until the age of 65. Workers working under difficult living conditions are provided for a professional pension and a partial pension. We provide special benefits for parents. For workers who continue to work under the conditions of early retirement, we give a 20% bonus to pay and we lower our contributions to employers who employ these workers. All those measures will be introduced gradually so that the transitional period will last for the next 15 years. We believe that the new proposal for pension insurance divides the burden evenly among all generations, although all the social partners do not agree. Therefore, we also expect a constructive debate during the adoption of this law and give the possibility for individual solutions to be further coordinated. Thank you very much.

Fourthly, each insured and pensioner shall be provided with direct access to all his past and current data collected at the pension institution through an online application. It is a technical solution that we believe requires a lot of creative work in preparing such an option, but the state must certainly introduce it if we want the pension system to be transparent to all. A number of arguments, and in particular examples from practice, convince us that it is not only the individual’s right to look at and regulate his own data relevant for the exercise of future rights specifically collected for each insured and pensioner, but also the need for a country that will enable the insured to look into the state of insurance and the payment of contributions, as well as the data managers, who will thus be able to regulate the insured’s data in an up-to-date manner and thus ensure a fairer range of rights. On the 1st point I have mentioned, the Slovenian People's Party's Group and our two parties have continued to draw attention to the pressing demographic problem affecting the pension fund since independence. Warned by calculations, the profession did the same. We have proposed measures of family policy to encourage young people to form a family and take care of their successors, who will also supplement the pension allowance. We have also proposed measures to ensure that the burden of parenting is not borne only by women, mothers, but also by fathers. In fact, all society must contribute to parental support. Therefore, we will also insist on the current pension reform in a fairer arrangement for mothers to be recognised by children for reducing the age limit at retirement. Such a positive arrangement has informed young people that the state appreciates their contribution to the country, society and, if you want social treasury and by having children, taking care of them and preparing them for the people, useful citizens. Parents, especially mothers, deserve the so-called "beneficiary internship", a professional pension if I express myself through the terminology of the new pension law, just as it is justified for others who work in difficult jobs, for soldiers, policemen, miners and the like. At the proposed age threshold of 65 years for six children, this means a reduction to only 61 years of age and even 12 children bring only a reduction to 57 years of age, which is still a few months less than now, while the rareness of families with such a large number of children and at the same time taking into account the necessary working age of 41 years, the financial consequences in the pension register are virtually undetectable. Despite the decline in age, the mother must have sufficient working time and therefore this proposal does not constitute a significantly higher burden on the cash register. Parenthood relief is not taken into account for an incomprehensible reason. According to the Slovenian People's Party, this is either discrimination or inconsistencies in the rules. If a person makes a conscious decision or is forced to retire prematurely because of different circumstances, he will, on the basis of a new proposal, receive certain minority in the calculation of the pension, but it is not reasonable that he should be punished at the same time by not recognizing the full benefit of the parent. We propose that the new pension law should stipulate that this right remains with all recipients and all those who have been granted the right to state pension to enforce the new law. All existing beneficiaries who would be allowed different rights by the new Act on Social Transfers should be allowed free choice or recognition of the right that is more favourable to them. All state pensions paid must be charged exclusively to the state budget as it has been so far. In order to make reform fair, we propose to seriously examine transitional periods and take into account numerous warnings and letters of protest from those who meet the conditions in the next few years. The transitional period is very strict and shows that the government literally wants to take out of the pockets of almost earned pensions. Example. A person who could retire in the first half of June following the old legislation, for example, will receive an additional four years under the new legislation. A specific case for a woman with 56 years of age completed this year, 35 years of working age, two children and two years of recognised study. Cases with such major differences between the old and the new legislation are not shown in the comparative calculations. This tough transition also leads people to take care of their rights as early as this year. On the basis of all these proposals, I propose an amendment to Article 393 to offer the possibility of retirement under the old legislation to all those who have a pension until the date of retirement. 12. For example, 2010 is 5 years or less. This will prevent the mass movement of people to the employment institution and will relieve the country. The people will take care of themselves at such a time until the day of retirement. We have received numerous indignant letters, where the affected require this right because they are just before retirement, which means that most of the working hours have already been served. Nothing will be solved because they feel cheated for a well - deserved pension and are forced to rush them into various ways to retire, to wait, and so forth. ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u99	Thank you. Mr Anton Urh will present the position of the Group of the Democratic Party of Pensioners.
ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u100	Mr Vice-President, distinguished Minister, Secretary of State, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! I will present the position of DeSUS Members on the proposal for a law on disability and pension insurance. After ten years of validity of the current pension system regime, which has not yet completed the gradual and complete transition to the implementation of all legal solutions, we have in the legislative procedure a new modernisation of the pension system. It is comparatively much more striking, most in terms of the question of what it means and what it brings in the field of retirement conditions. This is a topic that understandably raises dissatisfaction and fear of those who will be most affected by the reform in view of the expectation of meeting the conditions under the applicable law for a short period of several years, of course in the sense that they will have to work a little longer if they want to have a full old pension. Let me say right now that we are in the DeSUS Group in relation to this, as the most controversial points of the reform for the representatives of the Labour Party show, the unified view that the transition to a longer active period of work is necessary because this is dictated by demographic conditions and projections, and the reality of a truly premature retirement based on life. As in the vast part of the developed world, life expectancy in Slovenia is increasing and the population is rapidly ageing due to low birth rates. The most numerous postwar generation is going to retirement. This represents a major challenge for the pension system and consequently for society as a whole. In assessing whether the exercise of the right to a pension is determined only by working time or by age, it is not possible to overlook that the combination of the two conditions has been decided by our company two decades ago. The arguments that prevailed at that time came to a much greater conclusion today, but, of course, also between European countries, no pension system is based solely on retirement age. We agree that conditions cannot remain the same as the expected life expectancy and year-on-year retirement periods are increasing. We must not overlook the very important fact that every later generation enters the labour market later and also into substantially changed working conditions as the current generations have entered. In addition, we have assessed that the contribution of older workers to employers and society as a whole is of exceptional importance, as their long-standing experience, knowledge and excellent working habits are passed on to younger generations. Despite the fact that in the work of the reform, which raises the full retirement age to 65 years, we have attached something to the applicant, but there is something to be said about such a high age. The uniformity of retirement at 65 years of age for full retirement is somewhat questionable, in our opinion, due to the different specificities and complexity of jobs according to individual types of activity and the only intensity of work related to the psychophysical abilities of each individual. Indeed, the applicant has somehow taken this into account when it provided for early and partial retirement for those working after the 1960s who would no longer be able to perform in full or in part, but will still be punished with a lower pension, with a permanent minority, because the work obligations could simply not be carried out anymore. It is also true that the applicant has also foreseen the possibility of applying a full old-age pension to those who entered the labour market early at the age of 17, and who are generally employed in industrial and certain service sectors. All the jobs and professions in which we see the questionability of workers to carry out their work and will not be protected by the possibility of a full old pension at the age of 60 and 58 and a pension age of 43 and 41 years, as they will not enter the labour market early due to vocational and secondary education. The possibility seen in good faith by the proposaler to extend work activity is also in retraining workers, and there remains an open question of job opportunities that would offer at least about equal pay standards. Another important aspect of this reform is that we are concerned about how to provide jobs in order to extend work activity, what and how to create new jobs, so that younger generations can be included in the labour market, and at the same time have a secure employment for the older generation. Therefore, if I conclude and sum up on the subject of extending working time, the proposal is fully understandable on the one hand and, on the other hand, there remains the fear that the desired effects will not – even more – there will be a need to see the side effects which, according to our assessment, will show in the area of physical and mental health and in the increase in the level of structural unemployment. A very delicate matter of the bill's proposal is the issue of the wage-retirement relationship. For the DeSUS Group, the issue of the pension coordination formula remains open. If pension coordination is not followed by wage increases, this simply means that pensions will fall significantly, according to the proposed formula 60:40, depending on wage growth, which means an increase in the wage-retirement ratio, the coordination of pensions by Swiss formula, or the formula that departs from the reconciliation of pensions with wage increases, resulting in a further deterioration of the relative income position of pensioners, of which 53% is already at the poverty threshold today. 53%! Among the elderly there are 43%. In the context of proposals for the coordination of pensions, which, of the variants of the modified Swiss formula, clearly bring about a reduction of the pressure on public funding expenditure, thus pursuing only the objective of reducing the expenditure of the pension fund, not the objective of a decent pension for all generations of pensioners. However, the reality must also be taken into account that wages have increased considerably over the past 10 years, by around 81% and pensions by around 56%, irrespective of coordination with wage growth. The reality is also that the cost of living has already been achieved and even exceeded the level of European prices, and wages are about half the value of comparable European wages. So it is expected that wages will increase in the long term. Thus, without appropriate coordination, further reductions in the ratio between average pensions and salaries are inevitable. In order to avoid the consequences of the deterioration of the relative income situation of pensioners, a fair way of coordinating is only a valid way, i.e. full, with wage growth. In the DeSUS Group, we are aware that it is necessary and that it is possible to reach a social consensus on what coordination and the relationship between salaries and pensions should be. Our proposal is already well known: with a proposal of 80:20 in favour of wages, we want to get closer to the real capacity of the pension fund and the principle of maintaining the value of pensions to wages. This is a proposal that can be accepted that the serious damage to pensioners will not suffer and that pension funds or public finances will remain sustainable. The coordination period itself is changing with the proposal and it is necessary to know that pension levels are also lower in terms of salaries and that coordination is not acceptable once a year. Delaying pension growth for wage growth also means lagging behind the purchasing power of pensioners for the purchasing power of the active population. Let us not forget that the bulk of pensions are returned to the economy during the month, so pensions are not put aside, as some think, when they consider pensions to be primarily a cost. Contributions. Notwithstanding the fact that the contribution rates and the amount of contributions are laid down by the law governing social security contributions, this law should provide that the contribution rates for pension and disability insurance are the same for employers and workers. In the past, some 15 years ago, the same rates of contribution were in force by 1996. From a total contribution rate of 31 %, the rate of contribution gradually decreased to 24.35 % today, but only for employers, so today the ratio is 15.5 % : 8.85 %. Such a measure should only apply temporarily until the levy on wages is lifted. The measure was also necessary to ensure and strengthen the competitiveness of the economy, to increase job creation. In all these years, the pension register has been reduced by several billion euros. This should therefore also be included among the causes of the pension crisis and the cash register. The State budget thus took over this part of the financing, and until then no budget was needed for the pension fund. Moreover, in the past, surplus pension funds have been channelled into the financing of several important state investments that had no connection with the purpose of the pension fund. Unfortunately, the return to the same level of contribution did not occur at the time of the conjuncture, and it is unlikely that the increase in the rate of contribution would not be on the spot for employers during the crisis. However, such a measure would be necessary, acceptable, and a gradual transition to equalisation of the contribution rate, to raise inflows to the pension register and thus to the sustainability of the pension allowance, not least its eternal autonomy in relation to other sources of funding. In addition, Slovenia is a rare example among European countries which have a half-lower contribution from the employer to the pension fund than the worker's contribution. The principles of the International Labour Organisation are based on the requirement of equal burden of workers and employers. And if, as regards the rise in retirement age and the formula for the coordination of pensions, we want to be aligned with other countries of the European Union, then there is no reason not to take account of the equality of the ratio of payments of contributions from insurers and employers. Furthermore, concerns and objections need to be raised regarding the extension of the period of inclusion of the basics for the calculation of the pension. With the extension of the average years taken into account for the calculation of the pension under the current regime, we agree, of course, as it is absolutely necessary, given certain groups of insurers and known cases of ingenuity, espies, free professions, including farmers, to extend the period taken into account for the calculation of the pension. It is clear that the short-term period causes anomalies and time manipulations by paying higher and lower contributions to criminal insurance are, as a rule, more or less constant payments by the other active population throughout the working life. On the other hand, such an enormous extension is questionable, in particular for the pension of those groups of the active population facing low incomes, a certain part or a significant part of the insurance period when they are not in a regular working relationship, for example at the Employment and Remuneration Institute or, for example, health insurance compensation and, for example, those who work longer hours over small work. Therefore, in a spirit of fairness and solidarity, it is essential that, through the selection of more favourable years and appropriate heights of years, an optimum solution be found that will burden all categories of insurers evenly, since the latter can be found at any time and for a certain period of time in a situation where income is significantly lower. It is recalled that the longer accounting period for the calculation of the pension rate and the borrowing of successive years in general means a fall in pension levels, irrespective of the higher rate and irrespective of the abolition of the valorisation of pensions, the new pensions with pensions already retired. According to some calculations, the pension base is expected to be about 13% lower on average. Therefore, our proposal would be a compromise in order to take 24 years into account, the most favourable years for which the best years are not necessarily covered in successive years. However, for a 24-year period, the pension base would still be around 5.5% lower. One more word about the basis for the payment of contributions. Article 145 lays down the basis of which only employees, farmers and shareholders pay the contributions. The highest insurance base from which contributions to these groups would be paid is, according to the proposal of the law, 3.5 times the average wage. This actually means introducing a social cap, which we object to, as this is clearly contrary to the principle of solidarity in the pension system. Where a social cap is introduced only for certain categories of insured persons, the question of equal treatment of others, i.e. persons in a working relationship, shall also be raised. In addition, the introduction of a social cap in one area may also trigger the introduction to others, such as health insurance. Also, a grave violation of the composition of the institution's board of directors of pension and disability insurance is not reasonable. In addition, we believe it would be useful to examine the possibility of reintroducing the duality of the system's management bodies, i.e. the assembly system that was in force before 2005 and, as others have after their mission, comparable institutions — Health Insurance, Mutual Health Insurance — the composition must necessarily maintain the representation of those who provide the bulk of the necessary funding for insurance. In addition, it is essential that representatives of individual stakeholders play an important role in the design and implementation of the pension and disability insurance system. It would make sense for the Council of the Institute to add a number of tasks, such as cooperation in the design of contribution rates and in the design of valorisation coefficients. It is true that the changing social and economic situation dictates the need to adapt and thus change the pension system, as a public and primary pillar whose mission is to provide a deserving safe and dignified autumn of human life. The general objectives that the bill seeks to achieve, long-term financial sustainability and decent pensions for all retired generations are acceptable and enjoy general support. However, the solutions that are intended to achieve these objectives remain open, as there is doubt that they will have satisfactory, fair and fair results. Above all, the question remains whether the reform of the pension system is sufficient to achieve the desired results and how long can only the reform of the pension system sustain public financial sustainability. For the latter, for the greater autonomy of the pension fund and for a more equitable and fair sharing of the burden of reform, it would be at the same time to examine other areas, labour legislation, tax legislation and, here, at least, to ensure that more consistent implementation of the pension fund is adequately filled. There were some concerns and dilemmas that we see in the Desus Group. In the case of the pension reform of the open, we also hope that the hearing will be considered and duly taken into account in the next reading of the law. By providing our support. Above all, a consensus will be reached on those affected by the reform, pensioners and employment. Otherwise, unless our comments are taken into account for the second reading of the law, there will be no support for this law from the parliamentary group. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u103	Thank you. Mr Ljubo Germič will present the position of the Group of Liberal Democrats of Slovenia.
ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u104	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Mr. Minister with a colleague, colleagues and colleagues! In 1999, the current Pension and Disability Insurance Act was adopted so-called PPIZ1. The 2000 reform worked, but according to current analyses also due to the financial economic crisis, this reform is no longer sufficient in the medium term. If we want to provide, at least on the medium term, a pension scheme that will fulfil its obligations to its insurers and will also be financially sustainable, the necessary measures taken by the Government by means of a proposal for a law, namely the ZPIZ2, are brought to Parliament. The result of the law now in force is also increasingly lower pensions, which especially pushed certain groups of pensioners over the age of 65 to the risk of poverty. The bill before us is thus based on two key objectives. The first is the financial sustainability of the pension system and the second is to provide decent pensions or to stop the fall of pensions, as more and more of the elderly lived on the brink or even below the edge of poverty, which is unacceptable for the Liberal Democrats. The financial sustainability of the system and the prevention of the further fall in pensions will be achieved by raising the actual retirement age and changing the way pensions are valorised, depending on the demographic situation and the financial and economic situation. A decent pension can be provided by an individual from a variety of ways called the three pillars. Each of the three pillars has a certain role to play, with a key part of the changes focused on the first, i.e. the public pillar, which is still the foundation of our pension system. The main message of modernisation of the pension system is that both the state and individual are responsible for maintaining and improving economic and social security. At the LDS Club, we support both the core objectives set by the Government in the design of pension adjustments. This law is understood as a search for a balance between the interests of young people coming to the labour market, the middle generation that bears the greatest burden of pensions. So those who are close to retirement and those who already enjoy a well-deserved pension. Pensions come from groups of different interests after employment, sex, age, status, and so forth. The compromise reached in the text of the proposed law is, in our view, satisfactory, although it seems, but there are also clear messages that many are dissatisfied. The key measures to achieve the objectives of the reform are: to increase the proportion of active insurers by extending the work activity and raising the actual retirement age, to maintain solidarity in the pension scheme, to establish the principle of dependency between payments and the pension scheme and to pay off, to establish greater transparency and transparency of the system. The problems of the current pension system have proved to be faster in the current financial crisis than anticipated, but even without the crisis, the system should be updated within a few years. Predicts show that we are at the edge of the sustainability of the current system in 2020 when the expenditure of the pension fund would be spent on 11.2 of the social gross product. However, in order to cover the pension fund, much more costly amounts should also come from the state budget than so far. The pension system can't be transformed into a different one overnight. Preparation, graduality and transitional periods are needed, so it is the responsibility of the Government of the present and future generations to undertake reforms immediately. We must be aware of this by all actors involved in amending pension legislation. At the LDS Club, we support the transitional periods proposed by the Government and, in our opinion, are necessary for a less stressful transition to the new system. In the grave, the problem of our pension system can be divided into two parts. There are not enough people involved, as a result of the late inclusion of young people in the labour market and the elderly are leaving the labour market too soon. The proportion of people aged between fifty-five and forty years among the working population was 32.8% in 2008, one of the lowest in the European Union countries. In addition, we have larger groups of people who do not pay contributions from the work they do. Many, however, who are involved in the system pay the lowest amounts of contributions because the system has so far made this possible. The problem in the Republic of Slovenia is increasing demographic movements, which show that we will have fewer and fewer populations to pay for the cash register. The ratio between the retired and the working population on short- and medium-term terms will not withstand pressure, so this ratio needs to be changed on both sides of the age curve included in the labour market. This means encouraging older people to remain in the labour market for longer periods of time and, at the same time, to include young people in the labour market as soon as possible. In the LDS, we support measures on the proposal for a law because we believe they are simply urgent and that we are addressing the right issues and dealing with them accordingly. At the same time, it still maintains solidarity as a fundamental principle and value. We agree with the rise in retirement age, because calculations and simulations have convinced us that this is one of the points of maintenance of the system in advance. Some would say that there are other solutions, such as an increase in the rate of contributions, but in the LDS Club, we believe that this would further burden the generation that is already bearing the greatest burden. Moreover, this would have a negative impact on the competitiveness of the Slovenian economy. The transition periods, which are very important for the gradual transition to a new arrangement, seem appropriate to us. It is important that the new proposal introduces or maintains a system of Malus and Bonuses that aims to encourage people to remain active for longer. An important incentive, for example, will be to pay 20% of the early retirement pension if the individual decides to continue working. For us in the LDS, stimulating marriage solutions are important, which encourage people to stay longer working. In increasing working age, however, we consider and point out that there are certain limitations. Human abilities are not limitless because there are biological, physiological, health and other limitations that need to be respected. Therefore, on the spot, they pointed out that we will also have to change certain social processes at conceptual level in order to achieve these objectives. We are convinced that it is essential to adapt jobs to the older population and be able to integrate into lifelong learning. These are the segments where employers, workers and the country face great challenges. Depending on the law before us in its close connection, it is also a priority to reduce unemployment and create quality and better paid jobs. In reducing retirement age, only the possibility of children is maintained, and all other exceptions are abandoned. We believe that this makes the system more transparent. At the parliamentary club, we encourage the maintenance of an exception in children where parental care is shown. It is also a matter of positive discrimination, as this right will continue to be exercised by women in the majority. As we have already done in the debates at the LDS, we are again questioning the recognition of compulsory military service as an opportunity to reduce retirement age. The new law does not allow for an option that includes the current law. We note that it was a legal obligation that could not be avoided, so it would be unfair for those who had survived a certain period of life in the compulsory service of the military service to be able to do so compared to those who were able to work during that period. We believe that there are unjustified differences, since those serving the military term will later meet the conditions for retirement. In the LDS, we support the extension of the period for determining the pension base for the current eighteen years for a longer period. Our reflection and consideration is whether it is necessary to take consecutive years or if it is possible to bring together the best years. In particular, in the younger population, it is not necessary that every next job is better, today's labour market times are changed. We also believe that the length of the pension base is still free for talks and an agreement that would be different and even shorter than the anticipated and successive 34 years. Of course, the most just solution would be to take the entire working period into account, as it would prevent all speculation. This task certainly represents a response to the further construction of the pension system or the next reform. At the LDS, we also support the determination of a higher percentage, which, according to the calculations presented, stops the fall in pensions because we believe that this reform is essential. We would like to stress that on the other hand, the labour market curves would also be able to set up more mechanisms. This is to allow for faster entry of young people into the labour market, mechanisms to stimulate employment, valuable working relationships and less diverse circuits in the form of seemingly self-employment, which appears to be an increase in entrepreneurship. Prolongation of working time is a necessary but not a sufficient measure. We are aware, however, that such measures cannot be placed only in the present proposal of the law, but that these measures must also be of a systemic nature in other areas. In the LDS, we very much welcome the measures taken to establish the principle of dependency between payments and payments. Since pension rights are derived from work, so far there has been a major problem in avoiding or paying low amounts to certain entities, for example, p.p., working through an copyright or retirement contract into a pension register. In view of the situation, it is necessary to extend the circle of insurers and to include all forms of work. This was also followed by the Ministry in the case of small work. It is also a welcome opportunity for voluntary inclusion in compulsory insurance. We agree with the direction set out in the proposal of the pension law that each work counts. It's one big piece of content in the marriage. In extending or increasing the insurance base for contributions, we stress in particular that the proposal for a law should also be followed by the implementation of other systemic measures that will prevent the system from playing. In doing so, we estimate that the insurance base system is set at 60% of the average wage in RS to be a fairer solution if we follow the principle that they should pay the basis for a pension that will not require social transfer to prevent the risk of poverty. In doing so, it should be pointed out that a transitional period is to be introduced by 2017 when we move gradually from 50 % to 60 % of the average wage. In the area of accounting and payment of contributions to the pension register, we support the novelty of the LDS Club, which prohibits the postponement of payments of contributions. Although the previous solution in the law that allowed this to happen had a good purpose, it had unfortunately been born. Without the employee’s knowledge, someone has approved the postponement of payments of contributions which form an integral part of the salary and which also derives from the rights. In our view, it is only possible to postpone payment if, on the other hand, the State transfers the weight of unpaid contributions and also provides for the recovery of unpaid contributions. However, we expect a systemic solution for companies in short-term difficulties and to ensure their continued success through other measures. There is also something to be said about the formula or coordination of pensions, which is also linked to the relationship between pensions and salaries. So with a question that needs to be given all the attention and attention. This is also followed by one of the two objectives of the law, which is to prevent the further deterioration of pensions. At the LDS, however, we reiterate our responsibility to bear the same burden of the future for all generations. Therefore, it would be unreasonable and unfair to propose this only to some. We are of the opinion that the formula for coordination according to the calculations presented to us is appropriate, but that, as Minister, he would like to emphasise, the "snatting" of the grave edges is yet another space, which we also agree with at the LDS Club. In doing so, we believe that an excessive derogation would constitute a threat to the entire proposed system, which would be irresponsible. Since the law follows the solution that pensions are to the maximum extent reflected in the contributions paid, it is appropriate to clear the pension allowance of all payments not linked to the contributions paid and to find an appropriate solution elsewhere. Thus, it is excluded from the pension scheme of a state pension, which moves during social transfers and does not end, which needs to be highlighted in particular. It will also be more appropriate, increasing from €180 to €450 if the person does not have other resources to support. It is very important for the LDS that the new law preserves the principle of solidarity, which also shows that the law preserves family, disability and widow's pensions and that the relationship between the highest and lowest pensions is maintained between 1 and 4. The first so-called public pillar is subject to the greatest changes in the law. It is the responsibility of the State to the greatest extent, since in our view it must be based on a fair distribution of burdens and intergenerational solidarity and must, as such, guarantee social security, i.e. a decent pension. A specific part of the law represents the occupational insurance or compulsory supplementary pension scheme, which must be designed in such a way as to enable each insured person to influence his or her pension through his or her salary during an active period, thereby increasing the low pension awareness and transparency of the operators themselves. This is an important change that will allow employees working in difficult and health-related jobs to be included in a specific capital-covered scheme of predetermined contributions. The amount of the occupational pension will therefore no longer be fixed but will depend on the amount of the funds raised in the special account of the fund member at the time of the exercise of the right to a professional pension. This is the same system as for compulsory pension insurance. On the other hand, the second and third pillars are intended to preserve the social position of the insured at the time of retirement and require accountability from the individual. The area of individual voluntary insurance is quite underdeveloped in Slovenia, so in the LDS Club we specifically call for an urgent consideration of additional incentives to increase the involvement of individuals and employers in this form of insurance. It is necessary to achieve such an increase in the share of the premiums involved, as well as the yield achieved, if we are to maintain not only the financial but also the social sustainability of the pension system, together with the pension from the first pillar. The main novelty of the individual insurance that we support in the LDS is the investment policy of the member's life cycle, which means that the member assumes an investment risk corresponding to his age. As the retirement day approaches, investment policy is more conservative. Investment policy is implemented by pension funds that can be formed as mutual, umbrella, critical or as a group of covert funds. An individual will be allowed to choose whether to be included in a minimum guaranteed return fund or in life-cycle funds. In conclusion, I would also like to point out that the whole European Union is facing similar challenges to pension reforms as we have seen in Slovenia. This year, the European Commission held a public consultation on pension systems, as many EU countries are introducing pension reform as a result of population ageing. Although we are in pain in the Liberal Democracy of Slovenia, we support the necessary reform. Simply because it is essential for the stability of the pension fund and to prevent the further fall of pensions. The procedure for preparing the pension reform has been lengthy. He started more than a year ago. We therefore believe that they do not take the lead in saying that there was not enough time to discuss and make comments. Of course, we want social dialogue to continue, as the law can be improved in the framework of the parliamentary procedure. At the same time, we will strive to upgrade the talks with successful arrangements, taking into account arguments and realistic options. At the same time, we would like to draw attention to the fact that today we are talking about a reform that is likely to need to be upgraded in the next 10 and 15 years. But today and in the future, the reform that affects each and every one of us must be balanced between all generations. Intergenerational solidarity, which applies to the first pillar of compulsory pension and disability insurance, is based on the flow of the system — those who work pay contributions for those who are retired. These two fields are indisputably interconnected and interdependent. So if one part is down, the other part is placed in an unsolvable position. We must have this in front of all those who will eventually decide on the law. Thank you very much.

Every day for five hours, every year for three months and every ten years for three years, the expected life expectancy has been extended in developed industrial companies, including Slovenia, since 1960. This trend continues. The second fact that is important in considering pension reform is the demographic structure of the population, particularly in Slovenia, where, due to low fertility, it becomes more rapid in favour of the older population than in some comparable countries. Already in 2018, it will grow from 16% to 19% in the next 10 years to 23%, which means that in 20 years it will increase by a bad half. In 50 years, this share will double. We express our support for the proposed law, but we will try to put forward the following comments and proposals on it: an extension of the transitional period for obtaining old-age pensions for those workers and workers who have started working early. We propose that the transitional period should only be extended by 4 months and not by 6 months per year. Another time. Reduction in the number of years to determine the pension base. The bill provides for a 34-year period as a basis for calculating the pension base. Despite the revised calculation of the pension base and the compensatory factor incorporated into the law, the extension of the number of years taken into account in the calculation of the pension base will have an impact on the level of the pension base of future pensioners and is expected to show a decrease of 12,11 % for men in the case of a 34-year period and 10.05 for women. According to the wage policy in Slovenia in the last period, which we are all well aware of, the pensions will be very low. We therefore propose that we find a compromise solution to determine the basis, for example 25 years, which will take into account the principle of greater fairness and the linkage of payments with benefits, as well as the preservation of an adequate pension for the majority of the population. Social Democrats join initiatives to preserve the provisions of the old law, which increases ratings for every child in the family. After all, every new child is a new mercenary in a pension box. We are kind of opposed to this proposal. We are proposing a different, better solution because the recovery of contributions is not the responsibility of workers, but the responsibility of state authorities. In the context of the debate on the proposal for a new law on pension and disability insurance, I would also like to draw attention to the problem related to the payment of contributions from employers and employees. We cannot solve this under the pension law, as regulating this issue is part of the Social Contributions Act. However, if we follow the principle of fairness, we cannot overlook the fact that the relationship between employees' and employers' contributions is unbalanced, 15.5 to 8.85. The total contribution rate for pension and disability insurance is 24,35. The contribution rate for employers has halved in the past, resulting from legislative changes. It was mainly linked to the introduction of a payroll tax, which we have recently definitively abolished. According to Eurostat, the share of social security contributions paid by employees is the highest among the 27 countries in the European Union and the share of employers' contributions among the three lowest in the same programme. Under better or more sustainable economic conditions, we should consider a legal change that will lead to an equalisation of the contribution rate between employers and employees. The principles of the International Labour Organisation set out in Article 71 of the ratified Convention 102 are also based on the requirement of equal burden of workers and employers. This measure should be amended, as we have already said, by the Law on Social Contributions in relation to how the economic situation in Slovenia will be resolved. Improvements in job protection, adaptation of jobs and working conditions of older workers, so-called age management, construction of workers with a friendly working environment, are prerequisites that staying in the workplace will not only force but also the desire of employees.
