Simple calculations, such as those that have been circulating around the world for some time now, have also helped. If a regular worker with a thousand euro a net salary of 370 euros per month instead of a five-year bonded bank, he would save 211 000 euros after 29 years. Instead of the pension he will receive after 29 years of 580 euros, after 29 years he will receive EUR 590 interest from the bank each month. In addition, he would be left with a principal whom he could devote to his children or to the purchase of a protected apartment. Therefore, counting people will be even more hiding their incomes after paying back their contributions than any income, and the grey economy will be even more. The Ministry was probably aware of this problem, and it announced in the beginning the gradual establishment of the so-called National Defined Contribution, the NDC, which is known mostly in the northern European countries as well as in Italy. It is a virtual personal account from which the insured person can at any time understand how much he has already contributed to the pension fund and what would be the pension if he retired prematurely or if he continued in the working relationship even after he has fulfilled all the conditions for retirement. Only such a model is transparent, fair and financially sustainable. Seen because the insured person knows the amount of the funds paid, is fair because the benefits depend on the contributions paid, and is financially sustainable as the revenues and expenditure are offset. At the first presentation of the law, the minister said, quote, "If we want to introduce NDC in a few years' time, the system should be seducted in this direction today". Today, there is no spirit or hearing about the NDC system. Why? Or is it because it would be difficult to do so? Let's see. Article 140 of the Act requires the institution to provide an informational calculation of the old or early retirement pension to an insured person who is 5 years old until early retirement. However, in the section on supplementary insurance, the law in Article 242 instructs the insurance manager to keep personal accounts of members of the pension fund. The Member's personal account, as the law says, must contain data on individual deposits, total value of savings and so on. However, by 31 January of the current year at the latest, the pension fund manager shall inform each member of the status of his personal account on 31 December of the previous year. If all of this is to be established in a legally established transitional period, it would also be possible to establish an NDC system or a virtual personal account. I hope that the Minister has not given up on this system because he would fear excessive transparency that would make it difficult to abuse the pension fund in various unjustified privileges. With the help of a virtual personal account, any insured person could also disclose and prevent the abuse of an employer who may be hiding the non-payment of contributions. The Kosovo Commission has already warned the government three years ago that, I quote, "we are getting more and more complaints from affected individuals and trade union representatives about the suspicion of corruption, stating that the competent state authorities do not allow them to see the data, or employers indicate calculated and paid social security contributions, in particular, compulsory pension insurance". So it's the anti-corruption commission. Today, in times of crisis, this problem has escalated so much that more transparency in the payment of contributions is needed. The establishment of a virtual personal account would also mean a healthy pressure on the pension system, in which not only the principle of social equality and solidarity must be established, but also the principle of economic justice. The amount of the pension must also be more dependent on the contributions paid, otherwise it will have no motivational role. Let me finish. The Slovenian Democratic Party is aware of the problem of unfavourable demographic movements and supports the basic purpose of the law to establish a sustainable pension system. The life expectancy of 60 years old men is now 19 years and 24 years. ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u119	Mr. Svetlik has the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u120	Mr Vice-President, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! Much has already been said, and let me explain something also in the light of the questions and proposals that have been made in this debate. First of all, as far as the calculations are concerned. Many calculations were made.They were sent and I hope you all got everything. I'm not quite sure, because I sensed it in the debate as if some of them hadn't come to you. If not, they are also available on the Ministry's home page. They are accessible to everyone. I would point them out because they were made at macro level by seeing the effects of the proposed pension reform on the pension fund, in particular the gross domestic product and its share in the financing of pensions or public expenditure and so on. And of course also on a micro level, these were made last, mainly to show what the proposed modernisation of the pension system means for individuals, for their pensions. They were made in the population of all pensioners in 2009. So they're accessible to you. I am sorry if someone has not seen them, because we have done this with the aim of checking whether we have really measured the instruments of this pension system well, given what this means for the individual who will be retired in the coming period. And these calculations show, as I have already indicated, that the pension under the new system will be higher by at least 2%, compared to the lowest income categories if we maintain today. And that's not something that we could trouble you with unless someone digs into this calculation and finds a mistake. We didn't, and we believe the experts who did it to do it well. Furthermore, this leads us to a question on which we are discussing a great deal, around the level of pensions. And the claim that because of the new pension system, the new pension law, then the population of pensioners and those who are yet to be retired will be completely at a disadvantage or, as some say, will be impoverished. This is simply not true in these calculations. To make it easier to understand when calculating the amount of the pension, three factors are taken into account; the first are actually years to calculate the pension base, which we extend in the proposal from eighteen to forty-four. In fact, the impact of this extension is negative. This means that the pension base is reduced, but at the same time the pension calculation is also affected by the dose percentage we place on 80% and will be fixed and will no longer be reduced to 72.5%. And we also fix the so-called equalization factor to 0.755, which can affect and actually affect early pensions today, on the reconciliation of pensions, by reducing them. And the effect of all these three factors gives the result that we are talking about higher pensions compared to the existing system. Of course, if we are now changing the values of these parameters, if, for example, we are seeking to shorten the period on which the pension base is calculated, then it is clear that pensions will be increased on this account, so that the amount of pensions in this account will increase. At the same time, however, we must not forget that this also means a higher cost for the pension fund. This is our second goal to make this cash sustainability so that we can provide permanent financing for pensions to all who are entitled to them. And so, of course, they have an impact on both of these objectives, on the sustainability of the pension fund, and in particular on the level of pensions, including other proposals that revolve around in this circle. To introduce a different formula of coordination to introduce ratings for the military, for parenthood and so on. Of course, there can be a lot of these proposals. However, each such proposal entails a certain cost, a fixed higher expenditure on the pension fund. And here we must ultimately weigh what will be the result, whether we will achieve both goals at the same time. This is our agreed objective, in fact, and we are committed to this and, of course, with individual proposals, we can act like it, but of course, the question is whether we will be able to achieve the goal. We will now further assess these objectives financially, which means that these measures will be further evaluated as proposed by individual applicants. And, of course, in the course of the debate, they also said exactly what is meant by changing the value of one and the other parameters in this system that we are proposing. Among other things, the assessment, which is a grave that is not accurate, but your sense of debate, I can say that if we shorten the period for the calculation of the pension base for five years, it costs us about 180 million at the annual level. This is the order of magnitude of these individual factors that affect both objectives. And on the basis of such evaluated possible different parameters, we will also be able to continue this discussion. The second thing to hold on to is the question that we often open up, how many more applications for retirement have been made recently because the system is changing. In 2009, 13 000 650 were retired in the first nine months. In 2010, in the same period, 14 thousand 700 are retired, amounting to a thousand 50 more. Talking about an enormous increase is inappropriate because it does not. We've got the data in front of us. So it's not true that they would trigger some kind of wave that's unsupervised or something. But it probably monitors any change because people think and think about what suits them more at a given time. The time accumulated in the past, which was also a question, is taken into account. He's not going to take this marriage away. It's not taking away this new system. What was paid is acquired forever. Why can't self-employed people retire full and only partially? There are two statuses that are excluded and we cannot be pensioners at the same time and at the same time be employed or self-employed. Part-time retirement, half-time either one or the other employee or self-employed, or possible and given as one of the opportunities for a gradual transition to another status. The question was also raised with regard to equal levels of contribution for employers and employees. That's probably a legitimate question. However, the following should be said if we were trying to establish the previous situation, when the rate of contribution was also higher for employers, then we estimate that in these circumstances it is not opportunistic, that it is not possible to raise additional labour costs which are already among the relatively high, if some claim to be the highest in the European area, which would have an impact on the economic competitiveness and also on the opportunities that we give to the medium-sized youth generation, which must then bear so much more costs, and so much less on the resources for development investments. In our view, this would be irresponsible in terms of the situation of the economy. Of course, if these two contributions were to be settled, which is a legitimate issue, it should be said that the current basis for determining the pension is calculated only from the payment of the employee’s contribution and that the decline or reduction of that contribution would then also have an impact on the amount of the pension. So we haven't decided at the moment to change this way, i.e. the contribution rate or not in absolute height, not in proportion. The question is, how do we imagine the work of the elderly? However, there is a great deal of misunderstanding in interpreting the effects of this law on the work of the elderly. I would like to point out that those well-known stories about how a natural worker, builder or any other worker in production will not be able to work until the age of 65, are incompetent because, as a rule, he started working before the age of 20 and will be a woman with 43 years of age or 41 years of retirement long before he was 65 years old. If someone starts at 20 years of age, they'll get enough retirement years at 63 years if, of course, they didn't have interruptions so they can retire normally. If he started working 20 years ago, he could have retired earlier. In addition, as we say, we offer these categories of people who are older, who are losing their workforce, other opportunities. I mentioned a partial retirement earlier. If eight hours of work is too much, it may be six hours easier to work, it may be four hours easier. And this is one of the possibilities that this system offers. So, a partial retirement. For the most demanding jobs, we still have a mandatory additional occupational insurance in the occupational pension scheme for all those working in specially exposed areas of work. At the same time, we have adopted, and the Government has already approved an active ageing strategy that specifically aims at adapting jobs, changing the practice of employers. We are also working hard to protect workers at work and health care. Active employment policies target the older population to a large extent. In preparation and now it will also be confirmed by the Government's proposal, which is also a proposal for tax relief for those who invest in the second pillar, additional tax reliefs. Labour legislation is being prepared and will be amended. Measures against black work, including the amendment of the law, are also being prepared. Specific measures are being prepared for this most vulnerable population and, of course, parallel measures to support the pension and disability insurance system after the new. Nor is it true that it would not be possible to acquire a retirement age. 5 years of retirement can be bought up at any time. Nor is it specified on the basis of what this sum can be made. This, ladies and gentlemen, means a five-year suspension of pension reform, nothing else. This does not mean just a five-year suspension of pension reform. But it will also take five years to introduce much more acute conditions, shorter transitional periods if we are to achieve the same goals that we are now setting, if we choose to do so. There won't be time, as we have it now, for a transition. During this time, we may then be faced with additional intervention laws that will allow us to patch up an existing system that is difficult to operate today. Pension reform is one of them too. Why is the contribution being raised, so that we deviate from the contribution to self-employed persons, from the lowest contribution which is now a contribution from the minimum wage and will be gradually changed in order to make a contribution of at least 60% of the average wage? First, I would say that this process will be gradual, starting only in 2013 and then after 2 percentage points until we reach 60%. Today we are at about 50% of the average wage. Simply because 60% of the average wage, i.e. the contribution from this salary, is the minimum basis that ensures that the minimum minimum pension is paid. Otherwise, we subsidize the pensions of all those who pay less than that. And there aren't so many of these in the system today. The invalidity that was exposed, why are we shutting it down? We do not abolish the rights of disabled persons in the field of pension and disability insurance. These rights are in the present law, only in this law, at least eight, to professional rehabilitation, to transfer, to shorter working hours, to temporary compensation, to disability benefits. Even if the employment relationship has been interrupted by a person of his own volition, to a partial refund, until the retirement period due to disability or personal circumstances and until the payment of contributions by the Republic of Slovenia. All these rights remain in the system. Disability, however, is something that has been introduced as a kind of rent for some individual’s physical weakness in monetary form. We believe that the issue of these problems that some people have because of, in addition to providing them with adequate disability security, must be addressed through a disability protection system with special measures to enable people to be integrated into the normal living and working environment. Not only do they get 50, 100 euros a month because they have some physical flaw that they've been given during their lifetime. Why is a study not considered a working age? Because he doesn't pay contributions. Because at that time an individual was financed from public funds because he acquired adequate knowledge, which also led to a higher salary after his employment. This was a public investment in his working ability, in his competence and, of course, he directly benefits from it. ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u210	Thank you for your word. Nice greetings to all present! The proposal to amend the law on the coordination of transfers to individuals and households is intended to eliminate unacceptable differences or confusion in the recognition of partial compensation for lost income to family assistants. The current situation in this area is that some family assistants receive a refund of EUR 734.15 gross, the second EUR 597.43 gross, the third EUR 578.55 gross and the fourth EUR 560 gross, i.e. a net range of EUR 438 to 524 for equal work, depending on the place of residence and the time of entry into the family assistant system. This is certainly unacceptable and unacceptable for a socially and regulated country. This situation is due to a lack of premeditated changes to the law, which have been enforced since 1. 1. 2007 The ambition of the proposed interventional amendment of the law is solely the equalization of the rights of family assistants regardless of the place of residence, i.e. the amount of the minimum wage. In doing so, it should be borne in mind that, as a rule, family assistants are those citizens who would otherwise be potential candidates for unemployed persons and thus burden other government budget items or would be potential candidates for home protection without a family assistant, which would significantly increase the burden of municipal budgets. In view of the current state of the public finances, we have agreed at a meeting of the parent labour body to compromise the level of the minimum wage to be achieved gradually, immediately following the implementation of the law at 90% of the minimum wage or €660.74 gross and after 31. 12. 2011 to the end of 2012 at 95 % of the minimum wage or EUR 697,44 gross or with 1. 1. 2013 in full amount of minimum wage. We also agree with the extension of the law or the addition of the parent's working body, which ensures equal treatment or equal pay for lost income to parents under the rules on parental protection, and, finally, it is important that social work centres have to decide again on the rights set out in this Title in accordance with the law, which will eliminate the anomaly and overturn the blame for the current state of affairs between municipalities and social work centres. Dear colleagues and colleagues, I expect you to support the necessary changes to the law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2010-10-19-SDZ5-Redna-21.ana.u222	Thank you very much for your words, Mr. Vice President. Say good-bye to everyone again! In 2004, the Social Protection Act introduced the Family Assistant Institute, which, according to LDS, is a positive solution for disabled and non-movable persons who can stay at home and be cared for by one of the domestics who acquires the status of family assistant. Payment costs were charged to local communities. The Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Social Protection Act introduced in 2007 a revised method of financing the work of family assistants, the rights of the family assistant are further financed by means of a disabled person up to the amount of his or her payment capacity and resources in the amount of the contributions of the debtors. With this amendment of the law, the number of family helpers has decreased, and in the last year, the number of family helpers has increased again slightly, moving between a thousand and a thousand 100. Growth is also partly due to an increase in unemployment. Efforts to regulate the status of family helpers have been under way for some time now, but they have not been fully successful so far, even though it is a group that relieves family distress and helps society as a whole to care for the sick and the unmovable. If there were no family assistant, many of these people would need institutional attention. As the population grows older, the pressure on such assistance will increase. In addition, the family assistant also makes it possible to employ more difficult categories of the population, and we are solving two problems at the outset. The proposed legal amendment regulates or coordinates the partial payment for lost income for family assistants provided for by the Social Protection Act in order to determine the amount of the minimum wage. This resolves the different adjustment of the amount and coordination of the partial loss of income payment between the two groups of family assistants as to when their employment contracts have been signed. We agree with the proposed solution, although it would be best to arrange this immediately, we agree that it will be done gradually. Therefore, it is necessary to harmonise the payment following the entry into force of the law in the first part, i.e. at 90% immediately upon the entry into force of the law, 95 at the end of the following year and fully settle at the end of 2012, when the amount of the partial payment for lost income would reach the minimum wage. I am talking about the Long-Term Care and Long-Term Insurance Act, which is to be prepared in 2013, as a right to a personal assistant. The Social Protection Act was introduced in 2004 by the Institute of Family Assistants. It allowed disabled and immobile persons to stay at home with the care of a person living at the same address. At the time of the introduction of this institute, the costs of financing the family assistants were charged to the municipalities. In 2007, the Social Protection Act introduced a revised way of financing their work, by which the rights of a family assistant are further financed by means of a disabled person up to the level of his or her capacity to pay and by means of the amount of the contributions of the debtors, thereby greatly reducing the number of family assistants, as we have already heard. In this way, the amount of partial loss of income payment resulting from the non-conformity of the minimum wage provided for by the Minimum Wage Act and the current Act in question will be eliminated. It has undoubtedly resulted in discriminatory treatment of the recipients of the partial payment, since the minimum wage is now differentiated in this case in grave cases to those who received the decision to carry out their work prior to the entry into force of the law in question and to those who received it afterwards. In its entirety, the current situation does not merely reflect the uncertainty and non-conformity of the amount of the partial loss of income payment; it is also a matter of complete non-transparency in payments, as some of the amounts we have received from those affected to the parliamentary group are irrelevant or inadequate in relation to the existing legislation. In the Group of Members, we do know that this is not a long-term solution to the elimination of these anomalies. We therefore expect the competent ministry and the government to submit a law on long-term care and home-care insurance, in accordance with the programme, which will fully regulate long-term care for beneficiaries who, due to the various chronic conditions, the consequences of injuries and disability, cannot take care of themselves, so they need the assistance of another person. Although it has been promised that the issue will be resolved by the adoption of the Social Protection Act, which we are also looking forward to by the beginning of the implementation of the Act.
